1→"""Ranking and grouping helpers for work queue selection."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.helpers import (
     6→    detail_dict,
     7→    is_review_finding,
     8→    is_subjective_finding,
     9→    primary_command_for_finding,
    10→    review_finding_weight,
    11→    scope_matches,
    12→    slugify,
    13→    status_matches,
    14→    subjective_strict_scores,
    15→    supported_fixers_for_item,
    16→)
    17→from desloppify.engine.planning.common import CONFIDENCE_ORDER
    18→from desloppify.state import path_scoped_findings
    19→
    20→
    21→def subjective_score_value(item: dict) -> float:
    22→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
    23→        detail = detail_dict(item)
    24→        return float(detail.get("strict_score", item.get("subjective_score", 100.0)))
    25→    return float(item.get("subjective_score", 100.0))
    26→
    27→
    28→def build_finding_items(
    29→    state: dict,
    30→    *,
    31→    scan_path: str | None,
    32→    status_filter: str,
    33→    scope: str | None,
    34→    chronic: bool,
    35→) -> list[dict]:
    36→    scoped = path_scoped_findings(state.get("findings", {}), scan_path)
    37→    subjective_scores = subjective_strict_scores(state)
    38→    out: list[dict] = []
    39→
    40→    for finding_id, finding in scoped.items():
    41→        if finding.get("suppressed"):
    42→            continue
    43→        if not status_matches(finding.get("status", "open"), status_filter):
    44→            continue
    45→        if chronic and not (
    46→            finding.get("status") == "open" and finding.get("reopen_count", 0) >= 2
    47→        ):
    48→            continue
    49→
    50→        item = dict(finding)
    51→        item["id"] = finding_id
    52→        item["kind"] = "finding"
    53→        item["is_review"] = is_review_finding(item)
    54→        item["is_subjective"] = is_subjective_finding(item)
    55→        item["effective_tier"] = (
    56→            4 if item["is_subjective"] else int(finding.get("tier", 3))
    57→        )
    58→        item["review_weight"] = (
    59→            review_finding_weight(item) if item["is_review"] else None
    60→        )
    61→        subjective_score = None
    62→        if item["is_subjective"]:
    63→            detail = detail_dict(finding)
    64→            dim_name = detail.get("dimension_name", "")
    65→            dim_key = detail.get("dimension", "") or slugify(dim_name)
    66→            subjective_score = subjective_scores.get(
    67→                dim_key, subjective_scores.get(dim_name.lower(), 100.0)
    68→            )
    69→        item["subjective_score"] = subjective_score
    70→        supported_fixers = supported_fixers_for_item(state, item)
    71→        item["primary_command"] = primary_command_for_finding(
    72→            item,
    73→            supported_fixers=supported_fixers,
    74→        )
    75→
    76→        if not scope_matches(item, scope):
    77→            continue
    78→        out.append(item)
    79→
    80→    return out
    81→
    82→
    83→def item_sort_key(item: dict) -> tuple:
    84→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension" or item.get("is_subjective"):
    85→        return (
    86→            int(item.get("effective_tier", 4)),
    87→            1,  # Subjective items sort after mechanical items within T4.
    88→            subjective_score_value(item),
    89→            item.get("id", ""),
    90→        )
    91→
    92→    detail = detail_dict(item)
    93→    review_weight = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
    94→    return (
    95→        int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3))),
    96→        0,
    97→        CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(item.get("confidence", "low"), 9),
    98→        -review_weight,
    99→        -int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   100→        item.get("id", ""),
   101→    )
   102→
   103→
   104→def item_explain(item: dict) -> dict:
   105→    effective_tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   106→
   107→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
   108→        return {
   109→            "kind": "subjective_dimension",
   110→            "effective_tier": effective_tier,
   111→            "subjective_score": subjective_score_value(item),
   112→            "policy": (
   113→                "Subjective dimensions are always queued as T4 and do not outrank "
   114→                "mechanical T1/T2/T3 items."
   115→            ),
   116→            "ranking_factors": ["tier asc", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"],
   117→        }
   118→
   119→    detail = detail_dict(item)
   120→    confidence = item.get("confidence", "low")
   121→    is_subjective = bool(item.get("is_subjective"))
   122→    is_review = bool(item.get("is_review"))
   123→    ranking_factors: list[str]
   124→    if is_subjective:
   125→        ranking_factors = ["tier fixed to T4", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"]
   126→    elif is_review:
   127→        ranking_factors = [
   128→            "tier asc",
   129→            "confidence asc",
   130→            "review_weight desc",
   131→            "count desc",
   132→            "id asc",
   133→        ]
   134→    else:
   135→        ranking_factors = ["tier asc", "confidence asc", "count desc", "id asc"]
   136→    explain = {
   137→        "kind": "finding",
   138→        "effective_tier": effective_tier,
   139→        "confidence": confidence,
   140→        "confidence_rank": CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(confidence, 9),
   141→        "count": int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   142→        "id": item.get("id", ""),
   143→        "ranking_factors": ranking_factors,
   144→    }
   145→    if is_review:
   146→        explain["review_weight"] = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
   147→    if is_subjective:
   148→        explain["policy"] = (
   149→            "Subjective findings are forced to T4 and do not outrank "
   150→            "mechanical T1/T2/T3 items."
   151→        )
   152→        explain["subjective_score"] = subjective_score_value(item)
   153→    return explain
   154→
   155→
   156→def tier_counts(items: list[dict]) -> dict[int, int]:
   157→    counts = {1: 0, 2: 0, 3: 0, 4: 0}
   158→    for item in items:
   159→        tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   160→        counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
   161→    return counts
   162→
   163→
   164→def choose_fallback_tier(requested_tier: int, counts: dict[int, int]) -> int | None:
   165→    available = [tier for tier, count in counts.items() if count > 0]
   166→    if not available:
   167→        return None
   168→    return min(available, key=lambda tier: (abs(tier - requested_tier), tier))
   169→
   170→
   171→def group_queue_items(items: list[dict], group: str) -> dict[str, list[dict]]:
   172→    """Group queue items for alternate output modes."""
   173→    grouped: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
   174→    for item in items:
   175→        if group == "file":
   176→            key = item.get("file", "")
   177→        elif group == "detector":
   178→            key = item.get("detector", "")
   179→        elif group == "tier":
   180→            key = f"T{int(item.get('effective_tier', item.get('tier', 3)))}"
   181→        else:
   182→            key = "items"
   183→        grouped.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
   184→    return grouped
   185→

    28→
    29→def _scorecard_subjective(
    30→    state: dict,
    31→    dim_scores: dict,
    32→) -> list[dict]:
    33→    """Return scorecard-aligned subjective entries for current dimension scores."""
    34→    return next_render_mod.scorecard_subjective(state, dim_scores)
    35→
    36→
    37→def _low_subjective_dimensions(
    38→    state: dict,
    39→    dim_scores: dict,
    40→    *,
    41→    threshold: float = 95.0,
    42→) -> list[tuple[str, float, int]]:
    43→    """Return assessed scorecard-subjective entries below the threshold."""
    44→    low: list[tuple[str, float, int]] = []
    45→    for entry in _scorecard_subjective(state, dim_scores):
    46→        if entry.get("placeholder"):
    47→            continue
    48→        strict_val = float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0)))
    49→        if strict_val < threshold:
    50→            low.append(
    51→                (
    52→                    str(entry.get("name", "Subjective")),
    53→                    strict_val,
    54→                    int(entry.get("issues", 0)),
    55→                )
    56→            )
    57→    low.sort(key=lambda item: item[1])
    58→    return low
    59→
    60→
    61→def cmd_next(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    62→    """Show next highest-priority queue items."""
    63→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    64→    state = runtime.state
    65→    config = runtime.config
    66→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    67→        return
    68→
    69→    stale_warning = check_tool_staleness(state)
    70→    if stale_warning:
    71→        print(colorize(f"  {stale_warning}", "yellow"))
    72→    skill_warning = check_skill_version()
    73→    if skill_warning:
    74→        print(colorize(f"  {skill_warning}", "yellow"))
    75→
    76→    _get_items(args, state, config)
    77→
    78→
    79→def _get_items(args, state: dict, config: dict) -> None:
    80→    tier = getattr(args, "tier", None)
    81→    count = getattr(args, "count", 1) or 1
    82→    scope = getattr(args, "scope", None)
    83→    status = getattr(args, "status", "open")
    84→    group = getattr(args, "group", "item")
    85→    output_format = getattr(args, "format", "terminal")
    86→    explain = bool(getattr(args, "explain", False))
    87→    no_tier_fallback = bool(getattr(args, "no_tier_fallback", False))
    88→
    89→    target_strict = target_strict_score_from_config(config, fallback=95.0)
    90→
    91→    queue = build_work_queue(
    92→        state,
    93→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
    94→            tier=tier,
    95→            count=count,
    96→            scan_path=state.get("scan_path"),
    97→            scope=scope,
    98→            status=status,
    99→            include_subjective=True,
   100→            subjective_threshold=target_strict,
   101→            no_tier_fallback=no_tier_fallback,
   102→            explain=explain,
   103→        ),
   104→    )
   105→    items = queue.get("items", [])
   106→
   107→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
   108→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
   109→    narrative = compute_narrative(
   110→        state,
   111→        context=NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="next"),
   112→    )
   113→
   114→    payload = next_output_mod.build_query_payload(
   115→        queue, items, command="next", narrative=narrative
   116→    )
   117→    payload["overall_score"] = state_mod.get_overall_score(state)
   118→    payload["objective_score"] = state_mod.get_objective_score(state)
   119→    payload["strict_score"] = state_mod.get_strict_score(state)
   120→    payload["scorecard_dimensions"] = scorecard_dimensions_payload(
   121→        state,
   122→        dim_scores=state.get("dimension_scores", {}),
   123→    )
   124→    payload["subjective_measures"] = [
   125→        row for row in payload["scorecard_dimensions"] if row.get("subjective")
   126→    ]
   127→    write_query(payload)
   128→
   129→    output_file = getattr(args, "output", None)
   130→    if output_file:
   131→        if next_output_mod.write_output_file(
   132→            output_file,
   133→            payload,
   134→            len(items),
   135→            safe_write_text_fn=safe_write_text,
   136→            colorize_fn=colorize,
   137→        ):
   138→            return
   139→        raise SystemExit(1)
   140→
   141→    if next_output_mod.emit_non_terminal_output(output_format, payload, items):
   142→        return
   143→
   144→    next_render_mod.render_queue_header(queue, explain)
   145→    strict_score = state_mod.get_strict_score(state)
   146→    if next_render_mod.show_empty_queue(queue, tier, strict_score):
   147→        return
   148→
   149→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   150→    findings_scoped = state_mod.path_scoped_findings(
   151→        state.get("findings", {}),
   152→        state.get("scan_path"),
   153→    )
   154→    potentials = state.get("potentials", {})
   155→    next_render_mod.render_terminal_items(
   156→        items, dim_scores, findings_scoped, group=group, explain=explain,
   157→        potentials=potentials or None,
   158→    )
   159→    next_render_mod.render_single_item_resolution_hint(items)
   160→    next_render_mod.render_followup_nudges(
   161→        state,
   162→        dim_scores,
   163→        findings_scoped,
   164→        strict_score=strict_score,
   165→        target_strict_score=target_strict,
   166→    )
   167→    print()
   168→
   169→
   170→__all__ = ["_low_subjective_dimensions", "cmd_next"]
   171→

1→"""State scoring, statistics, and suppression accounting."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from copy import deepcopy
     6→
     7→__all__ = [
     8→    "suppression_metrics",
     9→]
    10→
    11→from desloppify.core._internal.coercions import coerce_confidence
    12→from desloppify.engine._scoring.policy.core import matches_target_score
    13→from desloppify.engine._state.filtering import path_scoped_findings
    14→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel, ensure_state_defaults
    15→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import ScanCoverageRecord
    16→
    17→_EMPTY_COUNTERS = ("open", "fixed", "auto_resolved", "wontfix", "false_positive")
    18→_SUBJECTIVE_TARGET_RESET_THRESHOLD = 2
    19→
    20→
    21→def _resolve_lang_from_state(state: StateModel) -> str | None:
    22→    """Best-effort language detection from state (scan_history > lang_capabilities)."""
    23→    history = state.get("scan_history")
    24→    if isinstance(history, list):
    25→        for entry in reversed(history):
    26→            if isinstance(entry, dict):
    27→                lang = entry.get("lang")
    28→                if isinstance(lang, str) and lang.strip():
    29→                    return lang.strip().lower()
    30→    capabilities = state.get("lang_capabilities")
    31→    if isinstance(capabilities, dict) and len(capabilities) == 1:
    32→        only_lang = next(iter(capabilities.keys()))
    33→        if isinstance(only_lang, str) and only_lang.strip():
    34→            return only_lang.strip().lower()
    35→    return None
    36→
    37→
    38→def _count_findings(findings: dict) -> tuple[dict[str, int], dict[int, dict[str, int]]]:
    39→    """Tally per-status counters and per-tier breakdowns."""
    40→    counters = dict.fromkeys(_EMPTY_COUNTERS, 0)
    41→    tier_stats: dict[int, dict[str, int]] = {}
    42→
    43→    for finding in findings.values():
    44→        status = finding["status"]
    45→        tier = finding.get("tier", 3)
    46→        counters[status] = counters.get(status, 0) + 1
    47→        tier_counter = tier_stats.setdefault(tier, dict.fromkeys(_EMPTY_COUNTERS, 0))
    48→        tier_counter[status] = tier_counter.get(status, 0) + 1
    49→
    50→    return counters, tier_stats
    51→
    52→
    53→def _coerce_subjective_score(value: dict | float | int | str | None) -> float:
    54→    """Normalize a subjective assessment score payload to a 0-100 float."""
    55→    raw = value.get("score", 0) if isinstance(value, dict) else value
    56→    try:
    57→        score = float(raw)
    58→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
    59→        score = 0.0
    60→    return max(0.0, min(100.0, score))
    61→
    62→
    63→def _subjective_target_matches(
    64→    subjective_assessments: dict, *, target: float
    65→) -> list[str]:
    66→    """Return dimension keys whose subjective score matches the target band."""
    67→    matches = [
    68→        dimension
    69→        for dimension, payload in subjective_assessments.items()
    70→        if matches_target_score(_coerce_subjective_score(payload), target)
    71→    ]
    72→    return sorted(matches)
    73→
    74→
    75→def _subjective_integrity_baseline(target: float | None) -> dict[str, object]:
    76→    """Create baseline subjective-integrity metadata for scan/reporting output."""
    77→    return {
    78→        "status": "disabled" if target is None else "pass",
    79→        "target_score": None if target is None else round(float(target), 2),
    80→        "matched_count": 0,
    81→        "matched_dimensions": [],
    82→        "reset_dimensions": [],
    83→    }
    84→
    85→
    86→def _apply_subjective_integrity_policy(
    87→    subjective_assessments: dict,
    88→    *,
    89→    target: float,
    90→) -> tuple[dict, dict[str, object]]:
    91→    """Apply anti-gaming penalties for subjective scores clustered on the target."""
    92→    normalized_target = max(0.0, min(100.0, float(target)))
    93→    matched_dimensions = _subjective_target_matches(
    94→        subjective_assessments,
    95→        target=normalized_target,
    96→    )
    97→    meta = _subjective_integrity_baseline(normalized_target)
    98→    meta["matched_count"] = len(matched_dimensions)
    99→    meta["matched_dimensions"] = matched_dimensions
   100→
   101→    if len(matched_dimensions) < _SUBJECTIVE_TARGET_RESET_THRESHOLD:
   102→        meta["status"] = "warn" if matched_dimensions else "pass"
   103→        return subjective_assessments, meta
   104→
   105→    adjusted = deepcopy(subjective_assessments)
   106→    for dimension in matched_dimensions:
   107→        payload = adjusted.get(dimension)
   108→        if isinstance(payload, dict):
   109→            payload["score"] = 0.0
   110→            payload["integrity_penalty"] = "target_match_reset"
   111→        else:
   112→            adjusted[dimension] = {
   113→                "score": 0.0,
   114→                "integrity_penalty": "target_match_reset",
   115→            }
   116→
   117→    meta["status"] = "penalized"
   118→    meta["reset_dimensions"] = matched_dimensions
   119→    return adjusted, meta
   120→
   121→
   122→def _aggregate_scores(
   123→    dim_scores: dict, compute_health_score_fn
   124→) -> dict[str, float]:
   125→    """Derive the 4 aggregate scores from dimension-level data."""
   126→    mechanical = {
   127→        n: d
   128→        for n, d in dim_scores.items()
   129→        if "subjective_assessment" not in d.get("detectors", {})
   130→    }
   131→    return {
   132→        "overall_score": compute_health_score_fn(dim_scores),
   133→        "strict_score": compute_health_score_fn(
   134→            dim_scores, score_key="strict_score"
   135→        ),
   136→        "objective_score": compute_health_score_fn(mechanical),
   137→        "verified_strict_score": compute_health_score_fn(
   138→            mechanical, [REDACTED]
   139→        ),
   140→    }
   141→
   142→
   143→def _active_scan_coverage(state: StateModel) -> ScanCoverageRecord:
   144→    scan_coverage = state.get("scan_coverage", {})
   145→    if not isinstance(scan_coverage, dict) or not scan_coverage:
   146→        return {}
   147→
   148→    lang_name = state.get("lang")
   149→    if isinstance(lang_name, str) and lang_name:
   150→        payload = scan_coverage.get(lang_name, {})

    65→def _render_grouped(items: list[dict], group: str) -> None:
    66→    grouped = group_queue_items(items, group)
    67→    for key, grouped_items in grouped.items():
    68→        print(colorize(f"\n  {key} ({len(grouped_items)})", "cyan"))
    69→        for item in grouped_items:
    70→            tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))

    75→            )

    78→def is_auto_fix_command(command: str | None) -> bool:
    79→    cmd = (command or "").strip()
    80→    return cmd.startswith("desloppify fix ") and "--dry-run" in cmd

    83→def _effort_tag(item: dict) -> str:

   389→def _recompute_stats(
   390→    state: StateModel,
   391→    scan_path: str | None = None,
   392→    *,
   393→    subjective_integrity_target: float | None = None,
   394→) -> None:
   395→    """Recompute stats and canonical health scores from findings."""
   396→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   397→    findings = path_scoped_findings(state["findings"], scan_path)
   398→    counters, tier_stats = _count_findings(findings)
   399→    state["stats"] = {
   400→        "total": sum(counters.values()),
   401→        **counters,
   402→        "by_tier": {
   403→            str(tier): tier_counts for tier, tier_counts in sorted(tier_stats.items())
   404→        },
   405→    }
   406→    _update_objective_health(
   407→        state,
   408→        findings,
   409→        subjective_integrity_target=subjective_integrity_target,
   410→    )
   411→
   412→
   413→def _empty_suppression_metrics() -> dict[str, int | float]:
   414→    return {
   415→        "last_ignored": 0,
   416→        "last_raw_findings": 0,
   417→        "last_suppressed_pct": 0.0,
   418→        "last_ignore_patterns": 0,
   419→        "recent_scans": 0,
   420→        "recent_ignored": 0,
   421→        "recent_raw_findings": 0,
   422→        "recent_suppressed_pct": 0.0,
   423→    }
   424→
   425→
   426→def suppression_metrics(state: StateModel, *, window: int = 5) -> dict[str, int | float]:
   427→    """Summarize ignore suppression from recent scan history."""
   428→    history = state.get("scan_history", [])
   429→    if not history:
   430→        return _empty_suppression_metrics()
   431→
   432→    scans_with_suppression = [
   433→        entry
   434→        for entry in history
   435→        if isinstance(entry, dict)
   436→        and (
   437→            "ignored" in entry
   438→            or "raw_findings" in entry
   439→            or "suppressed_pct" in entry
   440→            or "ignore_patterns" in entry
   441→        )
   442→    ]
   443→    if not scans_with_suppression:
   444→        return _empty_suppression_metrics()
   445→
   446→    recent = scans_with_suppression[-max(1, window) :]
   447→    last = recent[-1]
   448→
   449→    recent_ignored = sum(int(entry.get("ignored", 0) or 0) for entry in recent)
   450→    recent_raw = sum(int(entry.get("raw_findings", 0) or 0) for entry in recent)
   451→    recent_pct = round(recent_ignored / recent_raw * 100, 1) if recent_raw else 0.0
   452→
   453→    last_ignored = int(last.get("ignored", 0) or 0)
   454→    last_raw = int(last.get("raw_findings", 0) or 0)
   455→    if "suppressed_pct" in last:
   456→        last_pct = round(float(last.get("suppressed_pct") or 0.0), 1)
   457→    else:
   458→        last_pct = round(last_ignored / last_raw * 100, 1) if last_raw else 0.0
   459→
   460→    return {
   461→        "last_ignored": last_ignored,
   462→        "last_raw_findings": last_raw,
   463→        "last_suppressed_pct": last_pct,
   464→        "last_ignore_patterns": int(last.get("ignore_patterns", 0) or 0),
   465→        "recent_scans": len(recent),
   466→        "recent_ignored": recent_ignored,
   467→        "recent_raw_findings": recent_raw,
   468→        "recent_suppressed_pct": recent_pct,
   469→    }
   470→

    def test_scan_path_includes_holistic(self):
        """Holistic findings (file='.') are always included regardless of scan_path."""
        f1 = _finding("holistic", file=".", tier=4)
        f2 = _finding("in_scope", file="src/a.py", tier=1)
        f3 = _finding("out_scope", file="other/b.py", tier=1)
        st = _state([f1, f2, f3])
        items = get_next_items(st, scan_path="src", count=10)
        assert len(items) == 2
        ids = {i["id"] for i in items}
        assert ids == {"holistic", "in_scope"}

    def test_review_findings_use_natural_tier(self):
        review = _finding(
            "review_item",
            detector="review",
            tier=3,
            confidence="high",
            detail={"dimension": "naming_quality"},
        )
        mech = _finding("mech_item", detector="smells", tier=3, confidence="high")
        st = _state(
            [mech, review],
            subjective_assessments={"naming_quality": {"score": 92}},
        )
        items = get_next_items(st, count=2)
        # Both are tier 3 — review findings use natural tier, not forced T1

    51→        item["id"] = finding_id
    52→        item["kind"] = "finding"
    53→        item["is_review"] = is_review_finding(item)
    54→        item["is_subjective"] = is_subjective_finding(item)
    55→        item["effective_tier"] = (
    56→            4 if item["is_subjective"] else int(finding.get("tier", 3))
    57→        )
    58→        item["review_weight"] = (
    59→            review_finding_weight(item) if item["is_review"] else None
    60→        )
    61→        subjective_score = None
    62→        if item["is_subjective"]:
    63→            detail = detail_dict(finding)
    64→            dim_name = detail.get("dimension_name", "")
    65→            dim_key = detail.get("dimension", "") or slugify(dim_name)
    66→            subjective_score = subjective_scores.get(
    67→                dim_key, subjective_scores.get(dim_name.lower(), 100.0)
    68→            )
    69→        item["subjective_score"] = subjective_score
    70→        supported_fixers = supported_fixers_for_item(state, item)
    71→        item["primary_command"] = primary_command_for_finding(
    72→            item,
    73→            supported_fixers=supported_fixers,
    74→        )
    75→
    76→        if not scope_matches(item, scope):
    77→            continue
    78→        out.append(item)
    79→
    80→    return out
    81→
    82→
    83→def item_sort_key(item: dict) -> tuple:
    84→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension" or item.get("is_subjective"):
    85→        return (
    86→            int(item.get("effective_tier", 4)),
    87→            1,  # Subjective items sort after mechanical items within T4.
    88→            subjective_score_value(item),
    89→            item.get("id", ""),
    90→        )
    91→
    92→    detail = detail_dict(item)
    93→    review_weight = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
    94→    return (
    95→        int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3))),
    96→        0,
    97→        CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(item.get("confidence", "low"), 9),
    98→        -review_weight,
    99→        -int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   100→        item.get("id", ""),
   101→    )
   102→
   103→
   104→def item_explain(item: dict) -> dict:
   105→    effective_tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   106→
   107→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
   108→        return {
   109→            "kind": "subjective_dimension",
   110→            "effective_tier": effective_tier,
   111→            "subjective_score": subjective_score_value(item),
   112→            "policy": (
   113→                "Subjective dimensions are always queued as T4 and do not outrank "
   114→                "mechanical T1/T2/T3 items."
   115→            ),
   116→            "ranking_factors": ["tier asc", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"],
   117→        }
   118→
   119→    detail = detail_dict(item)
   120→    confidence = item.get("confidence", "low")
   121→    is_subjective = bool(item.get("is_subjective"))
   122→    is_review = bool(item.get("is_review"))
   123→    ranking_factors: list[str]
   124→    if is_subjective:
   125→        ranking_factors = ["tier fixed to T4", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"]
   126→    elif is_review:
   127→        ranking_factors = [
   128→            "tier asc",
   129→            "confidence asc",
   130→            "review_weight desc",
   131→            "count desc",
   132→            "id asc",
   133→        ]
   134→    else:
   135→        ranking_factors = ["tier asc", "confidence asc", "count desc", "id asc"]
   136→    explain = {
   137→        "kind": "finding",
   138→        "effective_tier": effective_tier,
   139→        "confidence": confidence,
   140→        "confidence_rank": CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(confidence, 9),
   141→        "count": int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   142→        "id": item.get("id", ""),
   143→        "ranking_factors": ranking_factors,
   144→    }
   145→    if is_review:
   146→        explain["review_weight"] = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
   147→    if is_subjective:
   148→        explain["policy"] = (
   149→            "Subjective findings are forced to T4 and do not outrank "
   150→            "mechanical T1/T2/T3 items."
   151→        )
   152→        explain["subjective_score"] = subjective_score_value(item)
   153→    return explain
   154→
   155→
   156→def tier_counts(items: list[dict]) -> dict[int, int]:
   157→    counts = {1: 0, 2: 0, 3: 0, 4: 0}
   158→    for item in items:
   159→        tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   160→        counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
   161→    return counts
   162→
   163→
   164→def choose_fallback_tier(requested_tier: int, counts: dict[int, int]) -> int | None:
   165→    available = [tier for tier, count in counts.items() if count > 0]
   166→    if not available:
   167→        return None
   168→    return min(available, key=lambda tier: (abs(tier - requested_tier), tier))
   169→
   170→
   171→def group_queue_items(items: list[dict], group: str) -> dict[str, list[dict]]:
   172→    """Group queue items for alternate output modes."""
   173→    grouped: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
   174→    for item in items:
   175→        if group == "file":
   176→            key = item.get("file", "")
   177→        elif group == "detector":
   178→            key = item.get("detector", "")
   179→        elif group == "tier":
   180→            key = f"T{int(item.get('effective_tier', item.get('tier', 3)))}"
   181→        else:
   182→            key = "items"
   183→        grouped.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
   184→    return grouped
   185→
   186→
   187→__all__ = [
   188→    "build_finding_items",
   189→    "choose_fallback_tier",
   190→    "item_explain",
   191→    "item_sort_key",
   192→    "tier_counts",
   193→    "subjective_score_value",
   194→    "group_queue_items",
   195→]
   196→

   272→def _update_objective_health(
   273→    state: StateModel,
   274→    findings: dict,
   275→    *,
   276→    subjective_integrity_target: float | None = None,
   277→) -> None:
   278→    """Compute canonical score trio from dimension scoring."""

   282→
   283→    # Deferred import to avoid circular dependency with desloppify.scoring
   284→    # (scoring -> _scoring/results/core -> _scoring/subjective/core -> review -> state -> _state/merge -> _state/scoring)
   285→    from desloppify.scoring import (

   290→

   292→    if not merged:

   294→
   295→    subjective_assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments") or None
   296→    integrity_target = (
   297→        max(0.0, min(100.0, float(subjective_integrity_target)))
   298→        if isinstance(subjective_integrity_target, int | float)
   299→        else None
   300→    )
   301→    integrity_meta = _subjective_integrity_baseline(integrity_target)
   302→    if subjective_assessments and integrity_target is not None:
   303→        subjective_assessments, integrity_meta = _apply_subjective_integrity_policy(
   304→            subjective_assessments,
   305→            target=integrity_target,
   306→        )
   307→    state["subjective_integrity"] = integrity_meta
   308→
   309→    has_active_checks = any((count or 0) > 0 for count in merged.values())

    56→def _print_scan_header(lang_label: str) -> None:

    61→def _print_scan_complete_banner() -> None:

    70→def _show_scan_visibility(noise, effective_include_slow: bool) -> None:

    72→    if not effective_include_slow:
    73→        print(colorize("  * Fast scan — slow phases (duplicates) skipped", "yellow"))
    74→    if noise.budget_warning:

    77→        print(
    78→            colorize(

    86→def _show_coverage_preflight(runtime) -> None:
    87→    """Print preflight warnings when scan coverage confidence is reduced."""
    88→    warnings = getattr(runtime, "coverage_warnings", []) or []
    89→    if not isinstance(warnings, list) or not warnings:
    90→        return
    91→
    92→    for entry in warnings:
    93→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
    94→            continue
    95→        summary = str(entry.get("summary", "")).strip()
    96→        impact = str(entry.get("impact", "")).strip()
    97→        remediation = str(entry.get("remediation", "")).strip()
    98→        detector = str(entry.get("detector", "")).strip() or "detector"
    99→
   100→        headline = summary or f"Coverage reduced for `{detector}`."
   101→        print(colorize(f"  * Coverage preflight: {headline}", "yellow"))
   102→        if impact:
   103→            print(colorize(f"    Repercussion: {impact}", "dim"))
   104→        if remediation:
   105→            print(colorize(f"    Fix: {remediation}", "dim"))
   106→
   107→
   108→def cmd_scan(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   109→    """Run all detectors, update persistent state, show diff."""
   110→    try:
   111→        runtime = prepare_scan_runtime(args)
   112→    except LangRuntimeOptionsError as exc:
   113→        lang_cfg = resolve_lang(args)
   114→        lang_name = lang_cfg.name if lang_cfg else "selected"
   115→        print_lang_runtime_options_error(exc, lang_name=lang_name)
   116→        raise SystemExit(2) from exc
   117→    except ScanStateContractError as exc:
   118→        print(colorize(f"  {exc}", "red"))
   119→        raise SystemExit(2) from exc
   120→    orchestrator = ScanOrchestrator(
   121→        runtime,
   122→        run_scan_generation_fn=run_scan_generation,
   123→        merge_scan_results_fn=merge_scan_results,
   124→        resolve_noise_snapshot_fn=resolve_noise_snapshot,
   125→        persist_reminder_history_fn=persist_reminder_history,
   126→    )
   127→    _print_scan_header(runtime.lang_label)
   128→    if runtime.reset_subjective_count > 0:
   129→        print(
   130→            colorize(
   131→                "  * Subjective reset "
   132→                f"{runtime.reset_subjective_count} subjective dimensions to 0",
   133→                "yellow",
   134→            )
   135→        )
   136→    if runtime.expired_manual_override_count > 0:
   137→        print(
   138→            colorize(
   139→                "  * Expired provisional manual-override assessments: "
   140→                f"{runtime.expired_manual_override_count} dimension(s) reset to 0. "
   141→                "Use trusted `review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` to replace them.",
   142→                "yellow",
   143→            )
   144→        )
   145→    _show_coverage_preflight(runtime)
   146→
   147→    findings, potentials, codebase_metrics = orchestrator.generate()
   148→    merge = orchestrator.merge(findings, potentials, codebase_metrics)
   149→    _print_scan_complete_banner()
   150→
   151→    noise = orchestrator.noise_snapshot()
   152→
   153→    show_diff_summary(merge.diff)
   154→    show_score_delta(
   155→        runtime.state,
   156→        merge.prev_overall,
   157→        merge.prev_objective,
   158→        merge.prev_strict,
   159→        merge.prev_verified,
   160→    )
   161→    _show_scan_visibility(noise, runtime.effective_include_slow)
   162→    show_scorecard_subjective_measures(runtime.state)
   163→    show_score_model_breakdown(runtime.state)
   164→
   165→    target_value = target_strict_score_from_config(runtime.config, fallback=95.0)
   166→
   167→    new_dim_scores = runtime.state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   168→    if new_dim_scores and merge.prev_dim_scores:

   170→
   171→    warnings, narrative = show_post_scan_analysis(

   176→    )
   177→    orchestrator.persist_reminders(narrative)
   178→
   179→    write_query(

     8→from desloppify.app.commands.scan.scan_helpers import _format_delta

    16→
    17→def _consecutive_subjective_integrity_status(state: dict, status: str) -> int:
    18→    """Return consecutive trailing scans with the given subjective-integrity status."""
    19→    history = state.get("scan_history", [])
    20→    if not isinstance(history, list):
    21→        return 0
    22→
    23→    streak = 0
    24→    for entry in reversed(history):
    25→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
    26→            break
    27→        integrity = entry.get("subjective_integrity")
    28→        if not isinstance(integrity, dict):
    29→            break
    30→        if integrity.get("status") != status:
    31→            break
    32→        streak += 1
    33→    return streak
    34→
    35→
    36→def show_diff_summary(diff: dict):
    37→    """Print the +new / -resolved / reopened one-liner."""
    38→    diff_parts = []
    39→    if diff["new"]:
    40→        diff_parts.append(colorize(f"+{diff['new']} new", "yellow"))
    41→    if diff["auto_resolved"]:
    42→        diff_parts.append(colorize(f"-{diff['auto_resolved']} resolved", "green"))
    43→    if diff["reopened"]:
    44→        diff_parts.append(colorize(f"↻{diff['reopened']} reopened", "red"))
    45→    if diff_parts:
    46→        print(f"  {' · '.join(diff_parts)}")
    47→    else:
    48→        print(colorize("  No changes since last scan", "dim"))
    49→    if diff.get("suspect_detectors"):
    50→        print(
    51→            colorize(
    52→                "  ⚠ Skipped auto-resolve for: "
    53→                f"{', '.join(diff['suspect_detectors'])} (returned 0 — likely transient)",
    54→                "yellow",
    55→            )
    56→        )
    57→
    58→
    59→def show_score_delta(
    60→    state: dict,
    61→    prev_overall: float | None,
    62→    prev_objective: float | None,
    63→    prev_strict: float | None,
    64→    prev_verified: float | None = None,
    65→    non_comparable_reason: str | None = None,
    66→):
    67→    """Print the canonical score quartet with deltas."""
    68→    stats = state["stats"]
    69→    new = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    70→
    71→    if (
    72→        new.overall is None
    73→        or new.objective is None
    74→        or new.strict is None
    75→        or new.verified is None
    76→    ):
    77→        print(
    78→            colorize(
    79→                "  Scores unavailable — run a full scan with language detectors enabled.",
    80→                "yellow",
    81→            )
    82→        )
    83→        return
    84→
    85→    overall_delta_str, overall_color = _format_delta(new.overall, prev_overall)
    86→    objective_delta_str, objective_color = _format_delta(new.objective, prev_objective)
    87→    strict_delta_str, strict_color = _format_delta(new.strict, prev_strict)
    88→    verified_delta_str, verified_color = _format_delta(new.verified, prev_verified)
    89→    print(
    90→        "  Scores: "
    91→        + colorize(f"overall {new.overall:.1f}/100{overall_delta_str}", overall_color)
    92→        + colorize(
    93→            f"  objective {new.objective:.1f}/100{objective_delta_str}",
    94→            objective_color,
    95→        )
    96→        + colorize(f"  strict {new.strict:.1f}/100{strict_delta_str}", strict_color)
    97→        + colorize(
    98→            f"  verified {new.verified:.1f}/100{verified_delta_str}",
    99→            verified_color,
   100→        )
   101→    )
   102→    if isinstance(non_comparable_reason, str) and non_comparable_reason.strip():
   103→        print(colorize(f"  Δ non-comparable: {non_comparable_reason.strip()}", "yellow"))
   104→    # Surface wontfix debt gap prominently when significant
   105→    wontfix = stats.get("wontfix", 0)
   106→    gap = (new.overall or 0) - (new.strict or 0)
   107→    if gap >= 5 and wontfix >= 10:
   108→        print(

Perfect! Now I have enough information. Let me compile a comprehensive report:

## Comprehensive Investigation: Review/Holistic Findings in Tier 1

I've completed a thorough investigation of how tier values are assigned to findings, with special focus on review-type findings. Here are my findings:

---

### **1. How Tier Values Are Assigned to Findings**

Tier assignment depends heavily on the **detector type** and **confidence level**:

#### **Detector-Specific Tier Assignment:**

- **Standard detectors** (unused, logs, exports, smells, etc.): Assigned directly by detector code
- **Review findings**: Use the `review_tier()` function from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/shared.py:154-160`
  ```python
  def review_tier(confidence: str, *, holistic: bool) -> int:
      """Derive natural tier from review confidence and scope."""
      if confidence == "high":
          return 1 if holistic else 2
      if confidence == "medium":
          return 2 if holistic else 3
      return 3
  ```

- **Per-file review findings** (`detector="review"`): 
  - High confidence → **Tier 2** (line 109 in per_file.py)
  - Medium confidence → **Tier 3**
  - Low confidence → **Tier 3**

- **Holistic review findings** (`detector="review"` with `holistic=True`):
  - High confidence → **Tier 1** (line 162 in holistic.py)
  - Medium confidence → **Tier 2**
  - Low confidence → **Tier 3**

- **Concerns findings** (confirmed concern verdicts from holistic review):
  - Same `review_tier()` logic as holistic review findings

#### **Location of Tier Assignment:**

Files where `tier` is set on findings:
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/holistic.py:162` - Sets tier for holistic review findings
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/per_file.py:109` - Sets tier for per-file review findings
- Individual detector files in `desloppify/engine/detectors/` and `desloppify/languages/*/detectors/` - Set tier for all other detector types

---

### **2. T1 Description: "Auto-fixable (imports, logs, dead deprecated)"**

Located in: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/planning/common.py:5-10`

```python
TIER_LABELS = {
    1: "Auto-fixable (imports, logs, dead deprecated)",
    2: "Quick fixes (unused vars, dead exports, exact dupes, orphaned files, cross-tool imports)",
    3: "Needs judgment (smells, near-dupes, single-use, small cycles, state sync)",
    4: "Major refactors (structural decomposition, large import cycles)",
}
```

This description maps T1 to **clear-cut, mechanical fixes** — primarily import-related auto-fixers, debug logs, and dead code removal.

---

### **3. How Review/Holistic Findings Get Their Tier: YES, INTENTIONAL DESIGN**

**This is intentional design, not a bug.** Here's the reasoning:

#### **Holistic Review Findings → Tier 1 When High Confidence**

Review findings with **high confidence** and **holistic scope** are promoted to **Tier 1** because:

1. **Holistic = Codebase-wide impact**: A high-confidence holistic finding affects broad design patterns or architectural issues across the entire codebase
2. **High confidence = Human-reviewed certainty**: Unlike mechanical detectors that can have false positives, holistic findings are assessed by human reviewers (via LLM) with stated high confidence
3. **Design intent**: The `review_tier()` function explicitly uses `holistic=True` as a signal to elevate tier. High-confidence holistic findings get **T1**, while high-confidence **per-file** findings only get **T2**

#### **Evidence from Code:**

Line 156-157 in `holistic.py`:
```python
tier=review_tier(confidence, holistic=True),
```

vs. Line 109 in `per_file.py`:
```python
tier=review_tier(confidence, holistic=False),
```

#### **BUT: Test Comments Contradict This**

There's a **conflicting signal** in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/core/test_plan.py`:

```python
def test_review_findings_use_natural_tier(self):
    review = _finding(
        "review_item",
        detector="review",
        tier=3,
        confidence="high",
        detail={"dimension": "naming_quality"},
    )
    # Comment: "Both are tier 3 — review findings use natural tier, not forced T1"
```

This test comment suggests review findings should **NOT** be forced to T1, yet the actual `review_tier()` function DOES promote them to T1 when holistic + high-confidence.

---

### **4. Work Queue Tier Assignment and Display**

In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/ranking.py:55-56`:

```python
item["effective_tier"] = (
    4 if item["is_subjective"] else int(finding.get("tier", 3))
)
```

**Key behaviors:**

- **Subjective findings** (assessment dimensions) → **forced to T4** (not review-type, but subjective assessments)
- **Review findings** → Use their natural tier (as set by `review_tier()`)
- **All other findings** → Use their declared tier

So review findings don't get special treatment during queue building — they keep whatever tier was assigned at import time.

---

### **5. Is This a Bug or Intentional Design?**

**INTENTIONAL DESIGN, with possible ambiguity:**

1. **Holistic high-confidence findings get T1 by design** because:
   - They represent codebase-wide patterns assessed with high human confidence
   - The `review_tier(confidence, holistic=True)` API explicitly encodes this
   - The priority boost aligns with the idea that high-confidence holistic insights should be acted on urgently

2. **BUT the T1 label mismatch is real**:
   - T1 says "Auto-fixable" (mechanical, no judgment needed)
   - Holistic review findings are **subjective** (require human judgment on design patterns)
   - A T1 review finding contradicts the T1 definition

3. **Possible explanations for the mismatch**:
   - T1 description was written assuming only mechanical detectors would appear there
   - Holistic findings were added later, and the tier system wasn't updated
   - The test comment "not forced T1" suggests someone noticed this inconsistency

---

### **Summary Table: Tier Assignment by Finding Type**

| Detector Type | Per-File/Holistic | Confidence | Resulting Tier |
|---------------|------------------|------------|-----------------|
| unused, logs, exports | N/A | N/A | T1 (hardcoded) |
| Review | Per-file | High | T2 |
| Review | Per-file | Medium | T3 |
| Review | Per-file | Low | T3 |
| Review | Holistic | High | **T1** |
| Review | Holistic | Medium | T2 |
| Review | Holistic | Low | T3 |
| Concerns | Holistic | High | **T1** |
| Subjective assessment | N/A | N/A | T4 (forced) |

---

### **Key Files to Understand This Logic**

1. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/planning/common.py` — TIER_LABELS definition
2. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/shared.py:154-160` — `review_tier()` function (the core logic)

1→"""Load and validate review dimension payloads from JSON data files."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import copy
     6→import json
     7→from functools import lru_cache
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from desloppify.intelligence.review.feedback_contract import ensure_prompt_contract
    11→from desloppify.intelligence.review.dimensions.validation import (
    12→    parse_dimensions_payload,
    13→)
    14→
    15→_LANG_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3] / "languages"
    16→_LANG_DATA_SUBDIR = "review_data"
    17→_DATA_DIR = _LANG_DIR / "_framework" / _LANG_DATA_SUBDIR
    18→
    19→# Canonical filename for the unified dimensions payload.
    20→_DIMENSIONS_FILE = "dimensions.json"
    21→
    22→
    23→def _load_json_payload_from_path(path: Path) -> dict:
    24→    """Load a JSON payload from *path* and return a dict."""
    25→    try:
    26→        raw = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    27→    except OSError as exc:
    28→        raise RuntimeError(f"Unable to read dimensions payload: {path}") from exc
    29→
    30→    try:
    31→        payload = json.loads(raw)
    32→    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
    33→        raise ValueError(f"Invalid JSON in dimensions payload: {path}") from exc
    34→
    35→    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
    36→        raise ValueError(f"Dimensions payload must be a JSON object: {path}")
    37→    return payload
    38→
    39→
    40→def _lang_payload_path(lang_name: str, filename: str) -> Path:
    41→    """Resolve the per-language review payload path."""
    42→    return _LANG_DIR / lang_name / _LANG_DATA_SUBDIR / filename
    43→
    44→
    45→def _override_filename(filename: str) -> str:
    46→    """Convert ``foo.json`` to ``foo.override.json``."""
    47→    if not filename.endswith(".json"):
    48→        return f"{filename}.override.json"
    49→    return f"{filename[:-5]}.override.json"
    50→
    51→
    52→def _load_json_payload(filename: str) -> dict:
    53→    """Load a JSON payload from the shared language review-data directory."""
    54→    return _load_json_payload_from_path(_DATA_DIR / filename)
    55→
    56→
    57→def _validate_optional_string_list(value: object, *, context: str) -> list[str]:
    58→    """Validate an optional list of strings (empty allowed)."""
    59→    if value is None:
    60→        return []
    61→    if not isinstance(value, list):
    62→        raise ValueError(f"{context} must be a list of strings")
    63→    out: list[str] = []
    64→    for idx, item in enumerate(value):
    65→        if not isinstance(item, str) or not item.strip():
    66→            raise ValueError(f"{context}[{idx}] must be a non-empty string")
    67→        out.append(item)
    68→    return out
    69→
    70→
    71→def _apply_dimensions_override(
    72→    base_payload: dict,
    73→    override_payload: dict,
    74→    *,
    75→    dims_key: str,
    76→    context: str,
    77→) -> dict:
    78→    """Apply a language override payload to a base dimensions payload."""
    79→    if not isinstance(override_payload, dict):
    80→        raise ValueError(f"{context} must be a JSON object")
    81→
    82→    allowed = {
    83→        dims_key,
    84→        f"{dims_key}_append",
    85→        f"{dims_key}_remove",
    86→        "dimension_prompts",
    87→        "dimension_prompts_remove",
    88→        "system_prompt",
    89→        "system_prompt_append",
    90→    }
    91→    actual = set(override_payload)
    92→    extra = sorted(actual - allowed)
    93→    if extra:
    94→        raise ValueError(f"{context} has unsupported keys: {extra}")
    95→
    96→    out = copy.deepcopy(base_payload)
    97→
    98→    if dims_key in override_payload:
    99→        out[dims_key] = override_payload[dims_key]
   100→
   101→    dims = list(out.get(dims_key, []))
   102→    for dim in _validate_optional_string_list(
   103→        override_payload.get(f"{dims_key}_append"),
   104→        context=f"{context}.{dims_key}_append",
   105→    ):
   106→        if dim not in dims:
   107→            dims.append(dim)
   108→
   109→    remove_dims = set(
   110→        _validate_optional_string_list(
   111→            override_payload.get(f"{dims_key}_remove"),
   112→            context=f"{context}.{dims_key}_remove",
   113→        )
   114→    )
   115→    if remove_dims:
   116→        dims = [dim for dim in dims if dim not in remove_dims]
   117→    out[dims_key] = dims
   118→
   119→    if "dimension_prompts" in override_payload:
   120→        prompt_overrides = override_payload["dimension_prompts"]
   121→        if not isinstance(prompt_overrides, dict):
   122→            raise ValueError(f"{context}.dimension_prompts must be an object")
   123→        prompts = dict(out.get("dimension_prompts", {}))
   124→        for dim_name, prompt in prompt_overrides.items():
   125→            prompts[dim_name] = prompt
   126→        out["dimension_prompts"] = prompts
   127→
   128→    remove_prompts = _validate_optional_string_list(
   129→        override_payload.get("dimension_prompts_remove"),
   130→        context=f"{context}.dimension_prompts_remove",
   131→    )
   132→    if remove_prompts:
   133→        prompts = dict(out.get("dimension_prompts", {}))
   134→        for dim_name in remove_prompts:
   135→            prompts.pop(dim_name, None)
   136→        out["dimension_prompts"] = prompts
   137→
   138→    if "system_prompt" in override_payload:
   139→        out["system_prompt"] = override_payload["system_prompt"]
   140→    if "system_prompt_append" in override_payload:
   141→        suffix = override_payload["system_prompt_append"]
   142→        if not isinstance(suffix, str):
   143→            raise ValueError(f"{context}.system_prompt_append must be a string")
   144→        current = out.get("system_prompt", "")
   145→        sep = "\n\n" if current and suffix else ""
   146→        out["system_prompt"] = f"{current}{sep}{suffix}"
   147→
   148→    return out
   149→
   150→
   151→def _load_payload_for_lang(
   152→    lang_name: str,
   153→    filename: str,
   154→    *,
   155→    dims_key: str,
   156→) -> tuple[dict, str]:
   157→    """Load payload for a language using shared-base + optional language overlay.
   158→
   159→    Resolution order:
   160→    1) Start from shared base payload in ``lang/framework/review_data``.
   161→    2) If language override payload exists, patch the selected base.
   162→    """
   163→    base_payload = _load_json_payload(filename)
   164→    context = filename
   165→
   166→    lang_override_path = _lang_payload_path(lang_name, _override_filename(filename))
   167→    if lang_override_path.is_file():
   168→        override_payload = _load_json_payload_from_path(lang_override_path)
   169→        base_payload = _apply_dimensions_override(
   170→            base_payload,
   171→            override_payload,
   172→            dims_key=dims_key,
   173→            context=str(lang_override_path),
   174→        )
   175→        context = f"{context} + {lang_override_path}"
   176→
   177→    return base_payload, context
   178→
   179→
   180→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   181→# Canonical loaders — use these for new code.
   182→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   183→
   184→
   185→@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
   186→def load_dimensions() -> tuple[list[str], dict[str, dict[str, object]], str]:
   187→    """Load and validate the unified review dimension definitions."""
   188→    payload = _load_json_payload(_DIMENSIONS_FILE)
   189→    dims, prompts, system_prompt = parse_dimensions_payload(
   190→        payload, context_prefix=_DIMENSIONS_FILE
   191→    )
   192→    return dims, prompts, ensure_prompt_contract(system_prompt)
   193→
   194→
   195→@lru_cache(maxsize=16)
   196→def load_dimensions_for_lang(
   197→    lang_name: str,
   198→) -> tuple[list[str], dict[str, dict[str, object]], str]:
   199→    """Load unified review dimensions for a language (with lang override applied)."""

## Investigation Report: Score Impact Infrastructure Analysis

I've completed a thorough investigation of the score impact infrastructure in desloppify. Here are my findings:

### 1. `compute_score_impact()` Function Analysis

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_scoring/results/core.py` (lines 339–394)

**Inputs Required:**
- `dimension_scores: dict` — Current dimension scores with "score", "tier", "checks", "issues", and "detectors" keys
- `potentials: dict[str, int]` — Merged potentials mapping detector name → count (from `merge_potentials()`)
- `detector: str` — The detector to estimate impact for
- `issues_to_fix: int` — Number of issues to fix in that detector

**How It Calculates:**
1. Finds which dimension the detector belongs to (via `DIMENSIONS` registry)
2. Returns 0.0 if dimension not in scores or detector has no potential
3. Gets old weighted failures from the detector's current data
4. Calculates new weighted failures: `max(0.0, old_weighted - issues_to_fix * 1.0)`
5. Reconstructs the dimension score with new weighted failures
6. Uses `compute_health_score()` to get both old and new overall scores
7. Returns the delta (rounded to 1 decimal place)

**Key constraint:** The function requires valid `potentials` to be non-empty, or it returns 0.0 silently.

---

### 2. Potentials Population and Structure

**State Structure:**
```python
state["potentials"] = {
    "python": {
        "complexity": 42,
        "coupling": 15,
        "smells": 8,
        ...
    },
    "typescript": {
        "unused": 23,
        "smells": 5,
        ...
    }
}
```

**Population Flow:**

1. **Scan Generation** (`plan.generate_findings()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/planning/scan.py:83-94`):
   - Each detector phase returns a tuple: `(findings: list, potentials: dict[str, int])`
   - All phase potentials are merged via `all_potentials.update(phase_potentials)`
   - Result is a flat dict: `{"complexity": 42, "coupling": 15, ...}`

2. **Merging into State** (`_merge_scan_inputs()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/merge_history.py:27-42`):
   - `potentials` dict is keyed by language: `state["potentials"][lang_name] = potentials_dict`
   - If `merge_potentials=True`, it updates existing lang potentials; otherwise it replaces
   - This happens **per-language**, so multi-language projects have nested structure

3. **Adding stale_wontfix** (`run_scan_generation()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_workflow.py:358-363`):
   - After augmenting findings with stale wontfix, adds: `potentials["stale_wontfix"] = count`
   - This is added to the flat potentials dict before merging into state

---

### 3. Accessing Potentials for Impact Calculation

**Critical Pattern** (from `action_engine.py:35-58`):
```python
def _impact_calculator(dimension_scores: dict, state: StateModel) -> Callable:
    merged_potentials = merge_potentials(state.get("potentials", {}))
    if not merged_potentials or not dimension_scores:
        return lambda _detector, _count: 0.0
    # ... builds closure that calls compute_score_impact(scoring_view, merged_potentials, ...)
```

**The `merge_potentials()` function** (`detection.py:26-32`):
```python
def merge_potentials(potentials_by_lang: dict[str, dict[str, int]]) -> dict[str, int]:
    """Sum potentials across languages per detector."""
    merged: dict[str, int] = {}
    for lang_potentials in potentials_by_lang.values():
        for detector, count in lang_potentials.items():
            merged[detector] = merged.get(detector, 0) + count
    return merged
```

This means:
- If `state["potentials"]` is empty or missing → `merged_potentials` is empty
- If `state["potentials"]` has no entries for any language → `merged_potentials` is empty
- If only has stale_wontfix and it's keyed directly (not under a language key) → may not merge properly

---

### 4. When Potentials Might Be Empty or Missing

**Scenarios where impact shows 0 or displays nothing:**

1. **No scan completed**: If `scan` was never run, `state["potentials"]` won't exist → next renders no impact
2. **Fast scan with --skip-slow**: Slow phases (e.g., duplication) don't run → their potentials are absent
   - But mechanical dimensions still compute scores via `dimension_scores` 
   - `compute_score_impact()` will return 0.0 for detectors not in merged potentials
3. **Profile="objective" or "ci"**: Subjective phases skipped → no subjective potentials
4. **Multi-language project with stale_wontfix misalignment**: If stale_wontfix is added as a top-level key instead of under a language key, `merge_potentials()` expects nested dicts
5. **State corruption or manual edit**: Potentials keyed incorrectly

---

### 5. Existing Per-Item Score Impact in Output

**Currently Implemented in `next` command** (`next_parts/render.py:169-178`):
```python
# Score impact estimate
detector = item.get("detector", "")
if potentials and detector and dim_scores:
    try:
        from desloppify.scoring import compute_score_impact
        impact = compute_score_impact(dim_scores, potentials, detector, issues_to_fix=1)
        if impact > 0:
            print(colorize(f"  Impact: fixing this is worth ~+{impact:.1f} pts on overall score", "cyan"))
    except (ImportError, TypeError, ValueError, KeyError):
        pass
```

**This is shown per item in `next` output when:**
- `potentials` is truthy (passed from state)
- `detector` is present in the item
- `dim_scores` is present in state
- No exceptions during import/call
- Impact > 0 (silent if impact ≤ 0)

**NOT shown in `scan` command output** — there's no analogous display in scan reporting dimensions.

---

### 6. Scorecard Projection and Per-Dimension Impact

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/planning/scorecard_projection.py`

**What it does:**
- Maps scorecard display names to CLI keys for review --dimensions
- Returns subjective entries with score/strict/placeholder metadata
- Serializes scorecard rows for JSON/query outputs
- **Does NOT compute per-dimension impact or projected scores**

**It does NOT:**
- Call `compute_score_impact()`
- Project dimension scores after fixing N issues
- Show drag/contribution breakdown per dimension

---

### 7. Narrative Action Engine Usage of Impact

**Location:** `action_engine.py`

The action engine uses `compute_score_impact()` in three places:

1. **`_append_auto_fix_actions()`** (line 105): Computes impact for each detector with auto fixers
2. **`_append_reorganize_actions()`** (line 167): Computes impact for structural refactoring actions
3. **`_append_refactor_actions()`** (line 204): Computes impact for manual/refactor actions
4. **`_dominant_detector_impact()`** in `dimensions.py` (line 145): Estimates highest-impact detector in a dimension

All actions include `"impact": round(impact_for(detector, count), 1)` in their output.

---

### 8. Why `compute_score_impact()` Might Not Show Output

**Top Reasons:**

| Scenario | Symptom | Root Cause |
|----------|---------|-----------|
| No scan run | Impact never computed | `state["potentials"]` is empty/missing |
| Fast scan (--skip-slow) | Impact 0 for skipped detectors | Potentials absent for slow detectors |
| Impact ≤ 0 | Silent (no output) | The try/except silently catches and passes; or detector has high pass rate already |
| Potentials structure wrong | Impact 0 for all items | State corruption; stale_wontfix not nested under language key properly |
| dimension_scores empty | Impact never attempted | No dimension scores computed (no active checks in merged potentials) |
| Detector not in merged potentials | Impact always 0 | Detector ran but has 0 potential, or not in merged set |

---

### 9. Per-Dimension Impact/Projection Not Currently Shown

**In `scan` output:**
- Shows dimension scores, checks, issues, tier
- Shows mechanical/subjective breakdown
- Shows wontfix debt
- **Missing:** "If you fix all issues in dimension X, score would be Y → Z (+delta pts)"

**In `next` output:**
- Shows per-item impact (1 finding fixed)
- **Missing:** "If you fix all N findings in detector D, dimension X would go from A → B"

**In plan output:**
- Shows prioritized per-tier task lists
- **Missing:** Per-task impact on overall score

---

## Key Insights for Reliable Per-Item Score Impact

To show per-item score impact reliably in both `next` and `scan`:

1. **Always validate potentials availability:**
   - Check `state.get("potentials", {})` is non-empty after merge
   - Call `merge_potentials()` explicitly to flatten language structure
   - Warn if potentials missing or sparse (fast scan mode)

2. **Handle edge cases:**
   - Detector in findings but not in potentials → impact should be 0 (or omitted)
   - No dimension_scores → skip impact calculation entirely
   - Impact ≤ 0 → decide: show "negligible" or omit

3. **Potentials structure must be consistent:**
   - `state["potentials"]` = `{lang_name: {detector: count, ...}, ...}`
   - Always add stale_wontfix under a language key, not at top level
   - Validate structure in `_merge_scan_inputs()` before storing

4. **For per-dimension projection in scan output:**
   - Collect all detectors in each dimension
   - Sum their open issue counts
   - Call `compute_score_impact(dim_scores, potentials, detector, sum_of_open_issues)` for each
   - Show: "Dimension X could improve from YY% → ZZ% by fixing all open findings"

5. **Cache impact calculations:**
   - In action_engine, the `_impact_calculator()` closure is computed once per command
   - For scan reporting, compute once and pass through rendering chain to avoid redundant calls

## Summary of Findings

### **Issue 1: "Show subjective dimension dead end" — Why `show abstraction_fitness` hits the "No open findings" dead end**

The problem is a **dimension lookup mismatch**. Here's the exact flow:

**The Code Path:**
1. User types `show abstraction_fitness`
2. `cmd_show()` calls `resolve_show_scope()` which returns the pattern as-is
3. `load_matches()` searches `state["findings"]` for detector-named findings → **returns empty** (because subjective assessments aren't stored as findings with that detector name)
4. Falls back to `load_matches_for_dimension()` at line 101-103 in cmd.py
5. `load_matches_for_dimension()` builds a lookup from `scoring_mod.DIMENSIONS` (the **mechanical dimensions only**)
6. The lookup contains keys like `"file health"`, `"code quality"`, `"test health"`, `"security"`, `"duplication"` — but **NO `"abstraction_fitness"`**
7. So `load_matches_for_dimension()` returns empty

**Why the lookup fails:**
- `scoring_mod.DIMENSIONS` contains **only mechanical dimensions** (5 total: File health, Code quality, Duplication, Test health, Security)
- `abstraction_fitness` is a **subjective dimension** living in `desloppify.intelligence.review.dimensions.holistic.DIMENSIONS` (20 total)
- These are completely separate systems — subjective dimensions are scored via assessments, not mechanical detectors

**The Change 5 code (lines 108-130) should catch this, BUT:**
- Line 112 builds the lookup from `scoring_mod.DIMENSIONS` (mechanical only)
- It never sees subjective dimension keys like `abstraction_fitness`
- So `matched_dim` at line 113 is always None for subjective dimensions
- The code **never enters the block** that prints "this is a subjective dimension"

**The Root Cause:**
The Change 5 code assumes subjective dimensions will be found in `scoring_mod.DIMENSIONS` with a "subjective_assessment" detector, but they're not. Subjective dimensions are in a separate registry and findings use the detector name `"subjective_review"` (not `"subjective_assessment"`).

---

### **Issue 2: The `--explain` flag on `next` is "useless" — minimal context**

The problem is **the explain data only shows ranking metadata**, not **context about why this item matters**.

**Current Implementation (ranking.py lines 104-153):**
```python
def item_explain(item: dict) -> dict:
    # Returns:
    explain = {
        "kind": "finding",
        "effective_tier": effective_tier,
        "confidence": confidence,
        "confidence_rank": CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(confidence, 9),
        "count": int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
        "id": item.get("id", ""),
        "ranking_factors": ranking_factors,  # ["tier asc", "confidence asc", "count desc", "id asc"]
    }
```

**What gets printed (render.py lines 196-206):**
```
explain: ranked by tier=3, confidence=high, count=15, id=xyz. [Optional policy statement]
```

**Missing Context:**
1. **No dimension score context** — Users don't see `"This detector is in the Code quality dimension, which is currently at 72% (strict: 65%)"` 
2. **No comparative ranking** — Why does this T3/high/count=15 item rank above another T3/high/count=12?
3. **No impact estimate** — Fixed items show "Impact: fixing this is worth ~+2.1 pts" (line 176 in render.py), but this is only shown for ALL items, not highlighted in the explain
4. **No context about alternatives** — `--explain` doesn't show why the top T1 item was chosen over top T2

**Why agents found it useless:**
- The explain data is **purely mechanical** (tier, confidence, count, ID)
- It doesn't answer the strategic question: **"Why should I fix this instead of something else?"**
- Scoring context (dimension health, overall impact) is computed separately in payload but not surfaced in explain

---

### **Issue 3: Score legend condition is too restrictive for agent environments**

**Current Code (scan_reporting_summary.py lines 116-124):**
```python
scan_count = state.get("scan_count", 0)
if scan_count <= 1 or gap > 10:
    print(colorize("  Score guide:", "dim"))
    # ... prints all 4 score definitions
```

**The Problem:**
- Condition is `scan_count <= 1 OR gap > 10`
- For **mature codebases**, `scan_count` is typically 10+ and `gap` (wontfix debt) is usually 2-5 points
- So the legend **almost never shows** for mature code
- **But in agent environments**, this context is valuable — agents run fresh scans and need to understand scoring immediately

**Agent Detection Function Available:**
- `_is_agent_environment()` exists in scan_reporting_llm.py (lines 21-29)
- It detects: `CLAUDE_CODE`, `DESLOPPIFY_AGENT`, `GEMINI_CLI`, `CODEX_*`, `CURSOR_TRACE_ID`
- This can be reused to always show the legend in agent environments

**Why the legend matters for agents:**
- Fresh scans mean agents haven't learned the scoring system yet
- The legend explains the "north star" (strict score) in one place
- Without it, agents see "strict 65.2/100 ↓ 3.2" with no context about what "strict" means

---

## Key Architectural Insights

1. **Two separate dimension systems exist:**
   - **Mechanical dimensions** (5): in `scoring_mod.DIMENSIONS`, scored by detectors
   - **Subjective dimensions** (20): in holistic.DIMENSIONS, scored by review assessments
   - They don't overlap; change 5 code needs to query **both registries**

2. **Subjective findings use `"subjective_review"` detector:**
   - Not `"subjective_assessment"` (which Change 5 code checks for)
   - This is why the check at line 116 always fails

3. **`explain` data is built at queue-build time (ranking.py):**
   - It's included in each queue item's `"explain"` key
   - But it only contains ranking factors, not strategic context
   - To be useful, it needs dimension health + impact projection + comparative context

4. **Agent environment detection is already available:**
   - Can be imported and reused to conditionally show the legend always in agent environments
